<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WkPS3MSmg1sEtyL4ANmP7x`</summary>

````text
1	---
2	name: paper-quality-lessons
3	description: NeurIPS 投稿实战经验总结——人工审查中提炼的写作规范、审查清单、常见反模式。Paper Agent / Reviewer / Director 写论文或审查论文时加载。
4	allowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]

6	version: 1
7	tags:
8	  - writing
9	  - paper
10	  - quality
11	  - checklist
12	  - review
13	roles:
14	  - agent
15	  - reviewer
16	  - director
17	  - paper_agent
18	---
19	
20	# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）
21	
22	> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。
23	> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）
28	
29	### 1.1 数字必须有源头
30	
31	- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件
32	- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据
33	- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报
34	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance
35	
36	> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。
37	
38	### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用
39	
40	- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol
41	- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明
42	- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较
43	- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 "at unmatched volumes"）
44	
45	> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。
46	
47	### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活
48	
49	- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文
50	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复
51	- 术语修正后不可回退（如 "same norm" → "same per-layer standard deviation"）
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配
56	
57	### 2.1 Claim 强度规则
58	
59	| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |
60	|----------|--------------|
61	| 单 seed, 单数据集 | "preliminary observation", "initial evidence" |
62	| 单 seed, 多数据集 | "suggestive pattern", "indicative trend" |
63	| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 "we find" |
64	| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 "robust", "consistent" |
65	| 统计检验显著 | 可用 "significant", 附 p-value |
66	| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 "scale-invariant" 等 |
67	
68	### 2.2 禁止的过强表述
69	
70	- "remarkably stable" → 改为 "relatively stable" 或给出量化波动范围
71	- "confirmed" → 仅 "suggestive" 级别数据不能用
72	- "scale-invariant" → single-seed 验证不够
73	- "We attribute X to Y" → 如果是假说，必须改为 "We hypothesize" + "requiring causal verification"
74	- "roughly twice" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似
75	- "domain-agnostic" → 改为 "robust across N domains tested"
76	
77	### 2.3 Claim 降级机制
78	
79	当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：
80	- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落
81	- 硬 claim → 加 caveat 的软 claim
82	- 正文数字 → 移入 Appendix
83	
84	---
85	
86	## 三、叙事与 Storytelling
87	
88	### 3.1 结构原则
89	
90	- 核心 paradox/发现之间必须有**过渡句**桥接，不能让读者在关键处断线
91	- Introduction 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
92	- Discussion 不应重复 Results 的具体数字——引用表格编号
93	- 同一解释不要在多处重复推导——主文写一次 + 指向 Appendix
94	- Limitations 段不要写成一个 17 行大段——拆分为 2-3 个子段按主题分组
95	
96	### 3.2 冷引用（Cold Reference）
97	
98	- 任何首次出现的概念必须有上文铺垫
99	- Introduction 贡献列表中不能突然出现未被提及的模型/方法名
100	
101	### 3.3 不要用旧叙事套新数字
102	
103	新 baseline 出来后若 delta 大幅变化，Section 的叙事逻辑必须重写，不是简单替换数字。
104	
105	### 3.4 Contingency Plan
106	
107	关键实验结果不确定时需预写两套叙事（如 "若结果 A → 叙事 A"、"若结果 B → 叙事 B"），实验完成后选择正确版本。提交前必须确认只留了一套。
108	
109	### 3.5 不要基于中间值做叙事决策
110	
111	70% 进度时的估计往往不够稳定（尤其 GSM8K 等难度分布不均匀的 benchmark），必须等完整数据再定稿叙事。
112	
113	---
114	
115	## 四、术语与一致性
116	
117	### 4.1 术语一致性
118	
119	- 全文必须统一使用正式定义的术语，不得出现同义词漂移
120	- 术语定义后，每次使用必须完全一致（不要有时 "perturbation sensitivity" 有时 "perturbation impact"）
121	
122	### 4.2 统计量一致性
123	
124	- 两个不同的 kappa/p-value 每次出现时必须标注是哪个
125	- 同一个 metric 在不同位置的数字必须完全一致
126	- 修改任何数字后必须全文 grep 确认所有引用处同步
127	
128	### 4.3 p-value 格式
129	
130	- 无前导零、3 位小数：`p = .003`（不是 `p = 0.003`）
131	- 当非劣效性 p<0.001 实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明
132	
133	### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致
134	
135	Taxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 "no new content"，示例中就不能包含新信息。
136	
137	---
138	
139	## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）
140	
141	### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）
142	
143	提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：
144	```
145	XX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \placeholder{
146	```
147	所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。
148	
149	### 5.2 编译检查
150	
151	- [ ] 编译零 error
152	- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用
153	- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox
154	- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）
155	- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）
156	- [ ] Review 版启用行号
157	- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢
158	
159	### 5.3 数据完整性
160	
161	- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过
162	- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认
163	- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明
164	- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差
165	- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明
166	
167	### 5.4 图表检查
168	
169	- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配
170	- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）
171	- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt
172	- [ ] 小数精度全表统一
173	- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线
174	- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含
175	- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）
176	
177	### 5.5 引用检查
178	
179	- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）
180	- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级
181	- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配
182	- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目
183	- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）
184	- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确
185	- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐
186	
187	### 5.6 匿名化检查
188	
189	- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）
190	- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径
191	- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）
192	- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）
193	- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露
194	
195	### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）
196	
197	- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围
198	- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充
199	- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）
200	- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用
201	- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间
202	- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours
203	- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整
204	- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况
205	- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）
206	
207	### 5.8 叙事完整性
208	
209	- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 "under greedy decoding"）
210	- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 "single seed" 另一个已是 "10 seeds"）
211	- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留
212	- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆
213	- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）
214	- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）
215	- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）
216	
217	### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查
218	
219	- [ ] **`grep "—" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）
220	- [ ] **`grep " – " paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）
221	- [ ] 无 "-ing," 分词短语结尾（如 "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."）
222	- [ ] 无连续 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"（同段最多 1 个）
223	- [ ] 无 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."
224	- [ ] 无 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "a myriad of" / "a plethora of"
225	- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段
226	
227	### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对
228	
229	- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）
230	- [ ] `\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段
231	- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐
232	
233	---
234	
235	## 六、Reviewer 审查标准
236	
237	### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）
238	
239	- 论文数字与实验数据不一致
240	- Abstract 与正文矛盾
241	- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）
242	- 未经验证的数据写入论文
243	- 占位符残留
244	
245	### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）
246	
247	- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）
248	- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑
249	- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 "3×3×3=27" 但实际只有 15 格）
250	- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain
251	- 旧版数据引用
252	
253	### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程
254	
255	1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics
256	2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）
257	3. 独立重算所有衍生值
258	4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL
259	5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内
260	
261	---
262	
263	## 七、常见反模式速查表
264	
265	| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |
266	|--------|------|----------|
267	| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |
268	| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |
269	| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |
270	| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |
271	| 叙事超越数据 | "confirmed" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |
272	| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |
273	| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |
274	| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |
275	| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |
276	| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |
277	| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |
278	| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |
279	| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |
280	| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |
281	
282	---
283	
284	## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills
285	
286	写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：
287	- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式
288	- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计
289	- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范
290	- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训
291	
292	---
293	
294	## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训
295	
296	1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改
297	2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备
298	3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理
299	4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证
300	5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿
301	
````

</details>

      "content": "---\nname: paper-quality-lessons\ndescription: NeurIPS 投稿实战经验总结——人工审查中提炼的写作规范、审查清单、常见反模式。Paper Agent / Reviewer / Director 写论文或审查论文时加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 1\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - quality\n  - checklist\n  - review\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - reviewer\n  - director\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）\n\n> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。\n> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。\n\n---\n\n## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）\n\n### 1.1 数字必须有源头\n\n- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件\n- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据\n- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报\n- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance\n\n> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。\n\n### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用\n\n- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol\n- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明\n- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较\n- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 \"at unmatched volumes\"）\n\n> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。\n\n### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活\n\n- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文\n- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复\n- 术语修正后不可回退（如 \"same norm\" → \"same per-layer standard deviation\"）\n\n---\n\n## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配\n\n### 2.1 Claim 强度规则\n\n| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |\n|----------|--------------|\n| 单 seed, 单数据集 | \"preliminary observation\", \"initial evidence\" |\n| 单 seed, 多数据集 | \"suggestive pattern\", \"indicative trend\" |\n| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 \"we find\" |\n| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 \"robust\", \"consistent\" |\n| 统计检验显著 | 可用 \"significant\", 附 p-value |\n| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 \"scale-invariant\" 等 |\n\n### 2.2 禁止的过强表述\n\n- \"remarkably stable\" → 改为 \"relatively stable\" 或给出量化波动范围\n- \"confirmed\" → 仅 \"suggestive\" 级别数据不能用\n- \"scale-invariant\" → single-seed 验证不够\n- \"We attribute X to Y\" → 如果是假说，必须改为 \"We hypothesize\" + \"requiring causal verification\"\n- \"roughly twice\" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似\n- \"domain-agnostic\" → 改为 \"robust across N domains tested\"\n\n### 2.3 Claim 降级机制\n\n当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：\n- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落\n- 硬 claim → 加 caveat 的软 claim\n- 正文数字 → 移入 Appendix\n\n---\n\n## 三、叙事与 Storytelling\n\n### 3.1 结构原则\n\n- 核心 paradox/发现之间必须有**过渡句**桥接，不能让读者在关键处断线\n- Introduction 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Discussion 不应重复 Results 的具体数字——引用表格编号\n- 同一解释不要在多处重复推导——主文写一次 + 指向 Appendix\n- Limitations 段不要写成一个 17 行大段——拆分为 2-3 个子段按主题分组\n\n### 3.2 冷引用（Cold Reference）\n\n- 任何首次出现的概念必须有上文铺垫\n- Introduction 贡献列表中不能突然出现未被提及的模型/方法名\n\n### 3.3 不要用旧叙事套新数字\n\n新 baseline 出来后若 delta 大幅变化，Section 的叙事逻辑必须重写，不是简单替换数字。\n\n### 3.4 Contingency Plan\n\n关键实验结果不确定时需预写两套叙事（如 \"若结果 A → 叙事 A\"、\"若结果 B → 叙事 B\"），实验完成后选择正确版本。提交前必须确认只留了一套。\n\n### 3.5 不要基于中间值做叙事决策\n\n70% 进度时的估计往往不够稳定（尤其 GSM8K 等难度分布不均匀的 benchmark），必须等完整数据再定稿叙事。\n\n---\n\n## 四、术语与一致性\n\n### 4.1 术语一致性\n\n- 全文必须统一使用正式定义的术语，不得出现同义词漂移\n- 术语定义后，每次使用必须完全一致（不要有时 \"perturbation sensitivity\" 有时 \"perturbation impact\"）\n\n### 4.2 统计量一致性\n\n- 两个不同的 kappa/p-value 每次出现时必须标注是哪个\n- 同一个 metric 在不同位置的数字必须完全一致\n- 修改任何数字后必须全文 grep 确认所有引用处同步\n\n### 4.3 p-value 格式\n\n- 无前导零、3 位小数：`p = .003`（不是 `p = 0.003`）\n- 当非劣效性 p<0.001 实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明\n\n### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致\n\nTaxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 \"no new content\"，示例中就不能包含新信息。\n\n---\n\n## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）\n\n### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）\n\n提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：\n```\nXX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \\placeholder{\n```\n所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。\n\n### 5.2 编译检查\n\n- [ ] 编译零 error\n- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用\n- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox\n- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）\n- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）\n- [ ] Review 版启用行号\n- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢\n\n### 5.3 数据完整性\n\n- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过\n- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认\n- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明\n- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差\n- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明\n\n### 5.4 图表检查\n\n- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配\n- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）\n- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt\n- [ ] 小数精度全表统一\n- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线\n- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含\n- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）\n\n### 5.5 引用检查\n\n- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）\n- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级\n- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配\n- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目\n- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）\n- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确\n- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐\n\n### 5.6 匿名化检查\n\n- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）\n- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径\n- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）\n- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）\n- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露\n\n### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）\n\n- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围\n- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充\n- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）\n- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用\n- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间\n- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours\n- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整\n- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况\n- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）\n\n### 5.8 叙事完整性\n\n- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 \"under greedy decoding\"）\n- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 \"single seed\" 另一个已是 \"10 seeds\"）\n- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留\n- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆\n- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）\n- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）\n- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）\n\n### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查\n\n- [ ] **`grep \"—\" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n- [ ] **`grep \" – \" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）\n- [ ] 无 \"-ing,\" 分词短语结尾（如 \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"）\n- [ ] 无连续 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"（同段最多 1 个）\n- [ ] 无 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"\n- [ ] 无 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\"\n- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段\n\n### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对\n\n- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）\n- [ ] `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / 8-shot）\n- 未经验证的数据写入论文\n- 占位符残留\n\n### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）\n\n- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）\n- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑\n- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 \"3×3×3=27\" 但实际只有 15 格）\n- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain\n- 旧版数据引用\n\n### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程\n\n1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics\n2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）\n3. 独立重算所有衍生值\n4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL\n5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内\n\n---\n\n## 七、常见反模式速查表\n\n| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 {
  "text": "[写作优化反思] 深度分析报告——从 3.0 推向更高分\n\n## 战略诊断\n\n论文连续 4 轮 3.0 的核心瓶颈不是数据或分析不扎实，而是**叙事力不足**。三个层面：\n\n1. **Abstract 是数据摘要，不是故事**——7 行文本塞了 5+ 个数字和多个未解释的术语，reviewer 30 秒内无法判断 \"why should I care\"\n2. **防御性过度写作**——4 轮修改累积了大量重复 caveat，每个 claim 后紧跟 2-3 个限定，读者感觉作者自己不相信结果\n3. **关键洞察被淹没**——confidence margin mechanism、ensemble failure 等有说服力的内容被埋在长段落中或出现在错误位置\n\n与 McCoy (2020)、Marx (2020)、D'Amour (2022) 对比：这些论文以**概念**驱动叙事（\"BERT 模型真的一致吗？\"、\"当模型对个体预测不一致时意味着什么？\"），而我们以**测量**驱动叙事（\"这是 Jaccard 值，这是 α 值\"）。需要从 \"here are some values\" 升级为 \"here is what this means\"。\n\n---\n\n## 优化方案（13 项，按 impact 排序）\n\n### P1. [Abstract] 从数据摘要改为叙事型 ⚠️ Claim 呈现方式变化\n\n**问题**：开头两句是 generic setup + methodology（\"increasingly deployed, yet remains unquantified\"、\"Using...we evaluate...\"），findings 以术语堆积呈现（Jaccard, α, Bonferroni），缺少 WHY。\n\n**当前**（节选关键句）：\n> Hybrid linear recurrent architectures...are increasingly deployed, yet how attention affects per-example behavior across backbones remains unquantified. Using the controlled model zoo...we evaluate 10 benchmarks and decompose per-example effects on four...\n\n**建议**：\n> When attention layers are added to linear recurrent architectures, they help and hurt almost entirely different examples across backbones: pairwise overlap among three controlled hybrid designs is at most 10\\%, far below the 78--92\\% theoretical maximum. Using the model zoo of Wang et al. (2025)...we decompose per-example effects on four benchmarks and find that this predictive multiplicity depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$ for reasoning, $0.30$ for retrieval) rather than attention dose.\n\n**同时在 Abstract 末尾加入 ensemble failure**（从 Conclusion 提取）：\n> A proof-of-concept majority vote fails to outperform the best individual architecture despite 15--45\\% per-example disagreement, while oracle accuracy reveals 7--14\\,pp of untapped complementarity, suggesting that architectures interchangeable by aggregate metrics may carry hidden consequences for per-example reliability.\n\n**改进**：(1) 开头即 surprising finding；(2) 减少数字但保留关键量级；(3) 末尾传达实际意义\n\n---\n\n### P2. [Intro P1] 具体化 \"practical stakes\"\n\n**问题**：\"hidden consequences for deployment reliability and ensemble construction\" 太抽象，reviewer 不感受到紧迫感。\n\n**当前**：\n> ...architectures that score identically in aggregate may thus carry hidden consequences for deployment reliability and ensemble construction.\n\n**建议**：\n> ...architectures scoring identically in aggregate may nonetheless differ on 15--45\\% of individual predictions, meaning the choice of backbone determines which specific inputs a deployed system handles correctly.\n\n**改进**：用具体数字（15-45%）替代 abstract phrase，直接说 \"determines which specific inputs\"\n\n---\n\n### P3. [Intro L18] 拆分 monolithic findings 段落\n\n**问题**：L18 约 18 行，两个 findings + 统计数据 + 机制解释 + caveats 全在一段。Reviewer 快速扫读必然跳过大部分。\n\n**建议**：在 \"Attention-driven aggregate convergence is task-specific.\" 前分段。第二段作为 secondary observation 更简短。\n\n---\n\n### P4. [Throughout] 大幅减少 em-dash（~35 → ≤10） \n\n**问题**：全文约 35 个解释性 em-dash（`---`），paper-writing-norms §5.1 列为\"最显著的 AI 痕迹，审稿人最敏感\"。\n\n**典型替换**：\n| 当前 | 建议 |\n|------|------|\n| `different examples---pairwise Jaccard` | `different examples (pairwise Jaccard` |\n| `---exponential decay...delta rules (GDN)---creating` | 拆为两句或用冒号 |\n| `---a transition from 0.51...0.01---so` | 括号 |\n| `---a proxy for, not a direct measurement of` | 逗号或分号 |\n\n**操作**：全文 grep `---`，逐个替换为逗号/括号/分号/拆句。保留数值范围 `0.14--0.30` 和表格分隔符。\n\n---\n\n### P5. [Throughout] 降低防御性过度 hedging\n\n**问题**：同一个 caveat（single training run / seed vs architecture）出现 5 处：Intro C1、§4.3 两次、Limitations 两次。读者感觉作者不确信结果。\n\n**策略**：每个 caveat 首次完整说明，后续只用简短指针。\n\n**具体示例**：§4.3 中 \"Attributing the gap entirely to seed variation would require seeds to exceed the effect of replacing 25\\% of model layers with attention---implausible though not directly testable with the available data.\" → 删除。这个推理已在 Limitations 完整覆盖，正文重复属于防御性写作。\n\n---\n\n### P6. [§4.2] 给 confidence margin 机制添加段落标题\n\n**问题**：这是论文中最有机制洞察力的发现（attention rescues near-misses, disrupts marginals, with architecture-specific thresholds），但没有段落标题，被淹没在统计数据中间。\n\n**建议**：在 \"No surface-level feature...\" 前加 `\\paragraph{Confidence margin mechanism.}`\n\n---\n\n### P7. [Intro C2] 加强 C2 contribution framing ⚠️ Claim 强度变化\n\n**问题**：当前 C2 措辞极其谨慎（\"We further observe...This observation is dependent on GDN inclusion and does not replicate on other benchmarks.\"），读起来像\"附带发现了一个可能不可靠的东西\"。Reviewer 会问\"那为什么写进 contributions？\"\n\n**当前**：\n> \\item We further observe that on LAMBADA...This observation is dependent on GDN inclusion and does not replicate on other benchmarks.\n\n**建议**：\n> \\item On LAMBADA, we observe that aggregate cross-architecture variance and per-example overlap respond to attention on different timescales: the former decreases gradually while the latter saturates at the first attention level (\\S4.3). This suggests that attention narrows aggregate gaps through architecture-specific mechanisms rather than uniform rescue. The aggregate convergence component is dependent on GDN inclusion (\\S4.4).\n\n**改进**：(1) 删 \"further\"（弱化词）；(2) 先 WHAT → WHY → CAVEAT，而非 finding 和 caveat 交织；(3) \"does not replicate on other benchmarks\" 移到正文（contribution list 中太 defensive）\n\n---\n\n### P8. [§4.3 L80] 拆分 monolithic cross-scale 段落\n\n**问题**：L80 约 12 行，覆盖 5 个主题（attention weights、四 benchmark 独立性、cross-scale Jaccard、α inflation、340M convergence absence）。\n\n**建议**：在 \"Cross-scale analysis on all four benchmarks\" 前分段。\n\n---\n\n### P9. [§4.2 L51] 压缩 Multiplicity Variation 段落\n\n**问题**：~13 行包含基本发现、format independence、六 benchmark range、CI ordering、accuracy decorrelation、Jaccard uniformity、reasoning-task floor。信息过密。\n\n**建议删除/移入 appendix**：\n- \"among two-choice benchmarks alone, α spans 3× from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.41 (WinoGrande)...covering the full six-benchmark range within a single format\" → 移入 WinoGrande/BoolQ appendix\n- \"The near-identical α for PIQA (0.14) and HellaSwag (0.15) despite different evaluation formats suggests a shared reasoning-task floor\" → 压缩为从句\n\n估计节省 3-4 行，释放 P6 段落标题的空间。\n\n---\n\n### P10. [Conclusion] 在结果摘要和实践协议之间加概念总结 ⚠️ Claim 呈现方式变化\n\n**问题**：Conclusion 从数字直接跳到 protocol，缺少\"这意味着什么\"的概念层。\n\n**建议在 P1 和 P2 之间插入**：\n> These findings reveal that aggregate benchmark equivalence between hybrid architectures masks per-example specialization: each recurrent backbone, when augmented with attention, develops distinct competencies over individual inputs that are invisible to standard evaluation.\n\n**注意**：\"per-example specialization\"、\"distinct competencies\" 属于新措辞，需 Director 判断是否过强。数据支撑：~90% non-overlap（Jaccard ≤ 0.10）跨 4 benchmarks。\n\n---\n\n### P11. [Intro L3] 锐化 opening hook\n\n**问题**：以 temporal clause 开头（\"When attention layers are interleaved...\"），减弱冲击力。\n\n**当前**：\n> When attention layers are interleaved into recurrent architectures, they help and hurt almost entirely different examples---pairwise Jaccard overlap...\n\n**建议**：\n> Hybrid recurrent architectures that appear equivalent by aggregate benchmarks affect nearly disjoint sets of individual examples: adding attention to three controlled linear recurrent backbones produces helped and hurt sets with at most 10\\% pairwise overlap.\n\n**改进**：以 subject 开头；直接建立 aggregate vs per-example tension；删除一个 em-dash\n\n---\n\n### P12. [§4.1] 前置 finding，后置 caveats\n\n**问题**：§4.1 结构是 结果→caveat→caveat→机制→caveat→其他 benchmarks。LAMBADA finding 被 caveats 包围。\n\n**建议**：重组为 结果→机制→其他 benchmarks→caveats（集中在段末）。具体：将 \"Statistical power is inherently limited...\" 从结果报告紧后方移到段落末尾。\n\n---\n\n### P13. [Related Work] 添加差异化定位句\n\n**问题**：最后一句只说 WHAT（\"We show...produces analogous per-example multiplicity\"），没说 HOW DIFFERENT from prior work。\n\n**当前**：\n> We show that architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models produces analogous per-example multiplicity under matched training conditions.\n\n**建议**：\n> We extend this line of work to a new source of variation, the recurrent backbone itself, showing that architectural differences produce per-example multiplicity that varies systematically by task type under matched training conditions.\n\n---\n\n## Claim 强度变化汇总\n\n| 项目 | 变化类型 | 需审批 |\n|------|---------|--------|\n| P1 Abstract | 呈现方式改变（数字减少，叙事增强，加入 ensemble failure 数据） | ✅ |\n| P7 C2 | 删除 \"further\" + 重组 finding→mechanism→caveat 顺序 | ✅ |\n| P10 Conclusion | 新增 \"per-example specialization\" 概念句 | ✅ |\n| P11 Opening | 从观察型改为 tension 型开头 | ✅ |\n| 其余 9 项 | 纯结构/措辞优化，不涉及 claim 强度 | 不需要 |\n\n## 预期效果\n\n- **P1+P11**：reviewer 在 30 秒内能判断论文价值，解决 \"so what?\" 问题\n- **P3+P6+P8**：关键 finding 不再被淹没，改善 scannability\n- **P4**：消除最显著的 AI 写作标志\n- **P5+P12**：降低防御感，增加 confidence\n- **P7+P10**：C2 不再读起来像废话，Conclusion 有概念深度\n- **P9**：释放空间给其他优化\n\n等待 Director 审批后执行。",

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. H3 \citep{fu2023hungry} achieves near-Transformer quality with gated convolutions and SSMs, establishing an early design point in the hybrid space. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear attention \citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2.
8	
9	\paragraph{Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity.}
10	\citet{damour2022underspecification} formalized underspecification: equivalent aggregate performance can mask per-prediction divergence. Concrete NLP instances include seed-dependent BERT disagreement \citep{mccoy2020berts,sellam2022multiberts} and the four-phase convergence pattern of autoregressive LMs \citep{fehlauer2025convergence}. \citet{marx2020predictive} introduced predictive multiplicity as a complementary lens, showing that near-optimal models produce conflicting per-example predictions; extensions address probabilistic classification \citep{watson2023predictive}, Rashomon capacity \citep{hsu2022rashomon}, reconciliation \citep{du2025reconciling,haghighat2026resolving}, and cross-model disagreement as a correctness signal \citep{gorbett2026crossmodel}. \citet{swayamdipta2020dataset} characterize examples via training dynamics (variability across epochs); our decomposition operates at deployment time across architectures with no training signal. \citet{black2022model} survey the model multiplicity landscape and \citet{cooper2024arbitrariness} connect predictive arbitrariness to fairness. These studies attribute divergence to initialization noise or the near-optimal set within a single architecture. We show that architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models produces analogous per-example multiplicity under matched training conditions.
11	
```

Mamba-2 \\citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention; Mamba-3 \\citep{lahoti2026mamba3} extends the design further, while \\citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \\citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \\citep{basu2026routing}. \\citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.\n\n\\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}\nOur three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \\citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \\citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \\citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules. These descend from S4 \\citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \\citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, respectively, and connect to linear attention \\citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2.\n\n\\paragraph{Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity.}\n\\citet{damour2022underspecification} formalized underspecification: equivalent aggregate performance can mask per-prediction divergence. Concrete NLP instances include seed-dependent BERT disagreement \\citep{mccoy2020berts,sellam2022multiberts} and the four-phase convergence pattern of autoregressive LMs \\citep{fehlauer2025convergence}. \\citet{marx2020predictive} introduced predictive multiplicity as a complementary lens, showing that near-optimal models produce conflicting per-example predictions; extensions address probabilistic classification \\citep{watson2023predictive}, Rashomon capacity \\citep{hsu2022rashomon}, reconciliation \\citep{du2025reconciling,haghighat2026resolving}, and cross-model disagreement as a correctness signal \\citep{gorbett2026crossmodel}. \\citet{swayamdipta2020dataset} characterize examples via training dynamics (variability across epochs); our decomposition operates at deployment time across architectures with no training signal. \\citet{black2022model} survey the model multiplicity landscape and \\citet{cooper2024arbitrariness} connect predictive arbitrariness to fairness. These studies attribute divergence to initialization noise or the near-optimal set within a single architecture. We show that architectural variation in hybrid recurrent models produces analogous per-example multiplicity under matched training conditions.\n",

      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 11
    }

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	When attention layers are interleaved into recurrent architectures, they help and hurt almost entirely different examples---pairwise Jaccard overlap among three hybrid designs is at most 0.10, approximately twice the independence baseline. This per-example divergence is invisible in aggregate metrics: on LAMBADA, cross-architecture standard deviation drops 79\% as attention increases (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$; the heuristic BCa 95\% CI excludes zero), but each architecture reaches similar accuracy by rescuing and disrupting different inputs. Practitioners increasingly choose between recurrent backbones based on aggregate benchmark scores; architectures that score identically in aggregate may thus carry hidden consequences for deployment reliability and ensemble construction.
4	
5	This tension arises in the context of a rapid architectural shift toward hybrid recurrent-attention models, which interleave attention layers within linear recurrent backbones. Jamba \citep{lieber2024jamba}, Griffin \citep{de2024griffin}, RWKV-6 \citep{peng2024eagle}, and RecurrentGemma \citep{botev2024recurrentgemma} each combine recurrence with attention using different mixing strategies. These systems now serve as production-grade alternatives to pure Transformers, and the practical question of how much attention to include is typically answered by comparing aggregate benchmark numbers. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide a controlled model zoo spanning three recurrent architectures, five attention ratios, and two parameter scales, which enables systematic study of how attention allocation affects these hybrid designs.
6	
7	Most evaluations of hybrid architectures compare aggregate benchmark scores and report that a given attention ratio achieves a certain accuracy averaged across tasks or averaged across architectures. This practice can mask substantial per-example behavioral diversity. \citet{marx2020predictive} formalized this risk as predictive multiplicity: a prediction problem admits competing models with conflicting predictions on individual inputs. \citet{damour2022underspecification} documented a related phenomenon, underspecification, showing that models trained from the same pipeline with different random seeds can disagree on individual predictions despite equivalent aggregate performance. \citet{mccoy2020berts} demonstrated a concrete NLP instance: BERT models trained from different random seeds diverge on specific linguistic constructions. These studies examine variation from initialization or training noise within a single architecture. Whether predictive multiplicity extends to hybrid recurrent architectures, where the source of variation is the recurrent backbone itself, remains unquantified. The question matters because practitioners increasingly choose between recurrent backbones based on aggregate benchmarks; if architectures that score identically in aggregate behave differently on individual inputs, such choices carry hidden consequences for deployment reliability and ensemble construction.
8	
9	We present the first systematic per-example analysis of how attention affects cross-architecture agreement in hybrid recurrent models. Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}, we compare three architectures (GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2) at five attention ratios (0\% to 25\%) and two scales (340M and 1.3B parameters). These three architectures represent distinct approaches to linear recurrence: gated delta rules, multi-scale exponential decay, and gated state expansion. We evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks spanning retrieval, comprehension, reasoning, and mixed task categories. On four benchmarks---LAMBADA ($N = 5{,}153$), HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$), PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$), and ARC-Easy ($N = 2{,}376$)---we conduct per-example decompositions, classifying each example as helped, hurt, or unchanged when attention is added, and measure cross-architecture overlap using Jaccard similarity. Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling} previews our central result: aggregate convergence and per-example divergence follow different trajectories as attention ratio increases.
10	
11	\begin{figure}[t]
12	\centering
13	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_decoupling_hero.pdf}
14	\caption{Aggregate convergence and per-example divergence are decoupled. (a) Cross-architecture standard deviation on LAMBADA decreases gradually with attention ratio at 1.3B (solid) but partially rebounds at 340M (dashed). (b) Pairwise Jaccard similarity of per-example helped/hurt sets saturates at the first attention level (4\%) and remains flat.}
15	\label{fig:decoupling}
16	\end{figure}
17	
18	Our analysis yields two findings. First, per-example predictive multiplicity varies in magnitude across tasks, forming a task-dependent variation. The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), while pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$). Within each benchmark, $\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$), but differs substantially across task types, ranging from 0.14 (reasoning) to 0.30 (retrieval). Attention-driven aggregate convergence is task-specific. Of 10 benchmarks, only LAMBADA exhibits monotonic reduction in cross-architecture variance ($\rho = -1.0$, BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic given $n = 3$, \S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}; cross-architecture $\sigma$ drops 79\%). LAMBADA \citep{paperno2016lambada} is a pure context-retrieval task that requires predicting the final word of a passage from long-range dependencies; attention provides direct token-level access to the full context, which offers a natural explanation for why convergence occurs on this task and not others. Benchmarks that involve reasoning or comprehension show no convergence, with all $|\rho| \leq 0.7$ and $p \geq .233$. The Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$ does not survive strict correction at $\alpha = 0.05$; the confidence interval, which excludes zero, provides a complementary characterization of the effect. This convergence is present at 1.3B but absent at 340M (Appendix~\ref{app:scale_convergence}). On LAMBADA, aggregate $\sigma$ decreases gradually ($0.024 \to 0.005$) while per-example Jaccard saturates at 4\% attention (${\sim}0.09$)---the two quantities are decoupled. The experiments that follow begin with convergence (\S\ref{ssec:task_convergence}) because it establishes the aggregate backdrop against which per-example divergence (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}) and decoupling (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}) are measured.
19	
20	Our contributions are as follows:
21	\begin{itemize}
22	    \item We show that architectural variation---under matched training conditions---produces structured per-example predictive multiplicity with a task-dependent variation ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) across four benchmarks in three recurrent backbones, with magnitude independent of aggregate convergence (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}, \S\ref{ssec:decoupling}, Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). Unlike seed-driven disagreement within a single architecture \citep{mccoy2020berts, damour2022underspecification}, this multiplicity arises under matched training conditions where the primary controlled variable is the recurrent backbone, though we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects with single training runs (Limitations).
23	    \item We further observe that on LAMBADA, aggregate $\sigma$ decreases gradually with attention while pairwise Jaccard saturates at the first attention level, suggesting that attention reduces the performance gap through architecture-specific mechanisms rather than uniform rescue of the same examples (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}). This observation is dependent on GDN inclusion (excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$) and does not replicate on other benchmarks.
24	\end{itemize}
25

```text
1	\documentclass[11pt]{article}
2	
3	\usepackage[review]{acl}
4	
5	\usepackage{times}
6	\usepackage{latexsym}

16	
17	\title{Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Architectures}
18	
19	\author{Anonymous}
20	
21	\begin{document}
22	\maketitle
23	
24	\begin{abstract}
25	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that interleave attention with recurrence are increasingly deployed, yet how attention affects per-example behavior across backbones remains unquantified. Using the controlled model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}---three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales (340M/1.3B), single training runs per configuration---we evaluate 10 benchmarks and decompose per-example effects on four (LAMBADA, HellaSwag, PIQA, ARC-Easy). Per-example predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} varies across tasks ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) while pairwise Jaccard of helped/hurt sets remains uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$); multiplicity magnitude depends on task type, not attention dose. Aggregate convergence, by contrast, is task-specific: only LAMBADA provides exploratory evidence for monotonic variance reduction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$), consistent with attention compensating for retrieval differences across recurrent backbones. On LAMBADA, aggregate variance decreases gradually while per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level---the two are decoupled.
26	\end{abstract}
27	
28	\input{sections/introduction}
29	
30	\input{sections/related_work}
31	
32	\input{sections/method}
33	
34	\input{sections/experiments}
35	
36	\input{sections/conclusion}
37	
38	\section*{Limitations}
39	
40	Our analysis has several limitations.
41	
42	\paragraph{Coverage.} Per-example analysis covers four main benchmarks; BoolQ and WinoGrande are supplementary (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}, \ref{app:winogrande}). The correlation between $\alpha$ and task accuracy is moderate but not significant ($\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$); HellaSwag (60\%, $\alpha = 0.15$) and PIQA (73\%, $\alpha = 0.14$) show low $\alpha$ despite varied accuracy, while benchmarks near chance level (BoolQ, WinoGrande) show the highest $\alpha$. Accuracy alone does not explain the variation, though we cannot fully rule out more complex interactions. We examine three recurrent architectures from one model zoo; the controlled design (shared training data, tokenizer, hyperparameters) isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families.
43	
44	\paragraph{Methodological constraints.} With only $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable two-tailed $p$-value is approximately 0.017, contributing to the Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$ for LAMBADA not reaching conventional significance. Each zoo configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\times$ Jaccard gap (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence for architecture as the dominant factor. For context, \citet{mccoy2020berts} report that BERT models from different fine-tuning seeds disagree on ${\sim}5$--$7$\% of MNLI examples; however, their metric (pairwise prediction agreement) differs from ours (Jaccard of transition sets when adding attention), so direct numerical comparison is not meaningful. A proper calibration would require multiple pretraining seeds per architecture within this zoo, which is not available; without this baseline, we cannot bound the seed contribution to our observed cross-architecture disagreement. The within-architecture Jaccard (${\sim}0.33$) compares models at different attention ratios, not different random seeds; it therefore provides a proxy for, not a direct measurement of, architecture-versus-seed effects. Consequently, all cross-architecture per-example claims are conditioned on single training runs per configuration. At 340M, $\alpha$ is systematically higher than at 1.3B (by up to 8.4pp on ARC-Easy), but pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$), indicating that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity is more robust than its absolute magnitude. Our attention weight analysis is descriptive, not causal; it compares aggregate distributions and does not resolve whether per-example attention behavior differs on the specific examples where architectures diverge. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B)---a binary observation, not a scaling law.
45	
46	\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated after pre-training without fine-tuning; per-example effects and the decoupling pattern may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics such as log-probability or calibrated confidence may reveal different patterns of multiplicity.
47	
48	\section*{Ethics Statement}
49	
50	This work analyzes publicly available pretrained language models from the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}. No new data collection, human subjects research, or model training is involved. We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.
51	
52	\bibliography{references}
53	
54	\appendix
55	\input{sections/appendix}
56	
57	\end{document}
58

On LAMBADA, aggregate variance decreases gradually while per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level---the two are decoupled.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{sections/introduction}\n\n\\input{sections/related_work}\n\n\\input{sections/method}\n\n\\input{sections/experiments}\n\n\\input{sections/conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur analysis has several limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Coverage.} Per-example analysis covers four main benchmarks; BoolQ and WinoGrande are supplementary (Appendix~\\ref{app:boolq}, \\ref{app:winogrande}). The correlation between $\\alpha$ and task accuracy is moderate but not significant ($\\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$); HellaSwag (60\\%, $\\alpha = 0.15$) and PIQA (73\\%, $\\alpha = 0.14$) show low $\\alpha$ despite varied accuracy, while benchmarks near chance level (BoolQ, WinoGrande) show the highest $\\alpha$. Accuracy alone does not explain the variation, though we cannot fully rule out more complex interactions. We examine three recurrent architectures from one model zoo; the controlled design (shared training data, tokenizer, hyperparameters) isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families.\n\n\\paragraph{Methodological constraints.} With only $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable two-tailed $p$-value is approximately 0.017, contributing to the Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$ for LAMBADA not reaching conventional significance. Each zoo configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\\times$ Jaccard gap (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence for architecture as the dominant factor. For context, \\citet{mccoy2020berts} report that BERT models from different fine-tuning seeds disagree on ${\\sim}5$--$7$\\% of MNLI examples; however, their metric (pairwise prediction agreement) differs from ours (Jaccard of transition sets when adding attention), so direct numerical comparison is not meaningful. A proper calibration would require multiple pretraining seeds per architecture within this zoo, which is not available; without this baseline, we cannot bound the seed contribution to our observed cross-architecture disagreement. The within-architecture Jaccard (${\\sim}0.33$) compares models at different attention ratios, not different random seeds; it therefore provides a proxy for, not a direct measurement of, architecture-versus-seed effects. Consequently, all cross-architecture per-example claims are conditioned on single training runs per configuration. At 340M, $\\alpha$ is systematically higher than at 1.3B (by up to 8.4pp on ARC-Easy), but pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$), indicating that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity is more robust than its absolute magnitude. Our attention weight analysis is descriptive, not causal; it compares aggregate distributions and does not resolve whether per-example attention behavior differs on the specific examples where architectures diverge. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B)---a binary observation, not a scaling law.\n\n\\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated after pre-training without fine-tuning; per-example effects and the decoupling pattern may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics such as log-probability or calibrated confidence may reveal different patterns of multiplicity.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work analyzes publicly available pretrained language models from the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. No new data collection, human subjects research, or model training is involved. We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We analyze how attention affects cross-architecture performance variance using models from the hybrid recurrent model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}. Our analysis spans three architectures (GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2), five attention ratios (0\% pure recurrent, 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, 25\%), and two scales (340M and 1.3B parameters). In the h$k$-1 notation used throughout, one attention layer is interleaved per every $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 denotes 25\% attention. All models within each scale share identical training data and hyperparameters; they differ only in architecture and attention configuration. We evaluate on 10 benchmarks spanning retrieval, comprehension, reasoning, and mixed task categories, plus one supplementary benchmark (COPA, $n = 100$).
4	
5	\subsection{Task-Specific Convergence}\label{ssec:task_convergence}
6	
7	Table~\ref{tab:convergence} reports cross-architecture $\sigma$ at each attention ratio for all benchmarks at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA is the only benchmark exhibiting monotonic convergence: $\rho = -1.0$ ($p = .017$; BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic given $n = 3$ architectures, \S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}; $\sigma$ drops 79\%; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$; excluding GDN: $\rho = -0.7$, $p = .233$, \S\ref{ssec:robustness}). Statistical power is inherently limited with $n = 5$ ordinal levels, so this convergence is best interpreted as exploratory. This is consistent with attention compensating for differences in how recurrent architectures retrieve from context \citep{arora2024zoology}---LAMBADA requires predicting a final word from long-range dependencies, and attention provides direct token-level access that narrows architectural differences in recall capacity \citep{arora2024based}. The three recurrent backbones differ in how they compress sequential context---exponential decay (RetNet), gated expansion (HGRN2), and delta rules (GDN)---creating divergent retrieval capacities that attention bypasses via direct token access. For reasoning and comprehension tasks, the bottleneck lies in multi-step inference rather than context retrieval, so attention cannot similarly eliminate cross-architecture variance. No other benchmark shows significant convergence (all $|\rho| \leq 0.7$, $p \geq .233$; Figure~\ref{fig:task_specificity}). Full convergence discussion appears in Appendix~\ref{app:convergence_detail}. Scale analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:scale_convergence}) shows this convergence is present at 1.3B but absent at 340M.
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_task_specificity.pdf}
12	\caption{Spearman $\rho$ between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\sigma$ for each benchmark at 1.3B scale. LAMBADA ($\rho = -1.0$; BCa 95\% CI $[-1.0,\,-0.7]$, heuristic) is the only benchmark with a negative correlation whose confidence interval excludes zero. Benchmarks are grouped by task category.}
13	\label{fig:task_specificity}
14	\end{figure}
15	
16	\begin{table*}[t]
17	\centering
18	\caption{Cross-architecture convergence across benchmarks (1.3B scale). $\rho$: Spearman correlation between attention ratio and cross-architecture $\sigma$ across five configurations. $p$: exact permutation test (two-tailed, 120 permutations). $\sigma$ values reported $\times 10^{2}$ (accuracy percentage points). Ratio: $\sigma_{\text{pure}} / \sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$. \textbf{Bold}: only benchmark with $\rho = -1.0$.}
19	\label{tab:convergence}
20	\begin{tabular}{llrrrrr}

22	Benchmark & Category & $\rho$ & $p$ & $\sigma_{\text{pure}}$ & $\sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$ & Ratio \\
23	\midrule
24	\textbf{LAMBADA$^{\ddagger}$} & \textbf{Retrieval} & $\mathbf{-1.0}$ & \textbf{.017} & \textbf{2.41} & \textbf{0.49} & \textbf{4.87} \\
25	BoolQ & Comprehension & $0.2$ & .783 & 1.63 & 2.36 & 0.69 \\
26	ARC-C & Reasoning & $0.6$ & .350 & 0.96 & 1.32 & 0.72 \\
27	HellaSwag & Reasoning & $0.4$ & .517 & 1.17 & 1.22 & 0.96 \\
28	PIQA & Reasoning & $-0.5$ & .450 & 0.54 & 0.34 & 1.59 \\
29	WinoGrande & Reasoning & $0.1$ & .950 & 0.67 & 0.78 & 0.86 \\
30	ARC-E & Mixed & $-0.7$ & .233 & 1.00 & 0.13 & 7.70 \\

32	SciQ & Mixed & $0.1$ & .950 & 0.87 & 0.74 & 1.19 \\
33	XStoryCloze & Mixed & $-0.6$ & .350 & 0.82 & 0.55 & 1.49 \\

35	COPA$^{\dagger}$ & Reasoning & $-0.67$ & .267 & 2.63 & 0.47 & 5.57 \\

37	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\footnotesize $^{\dagger}$Supplementary ($n = 100$); excluded from multiple comparison correction.} \\
38	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\footnotesize $^{\ddagger}$GDN h3-1 uses 16 attention heads; all other configurations use 32 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}).} \\
39	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\footnotesize Bonferroni-adjusted $p = 6 \times 2/120 = 0.100$ (exact); 0.102 arises from rounding raw $p$ to .017 before multiplying.}
40	\end{tabular}
41	\end{table*}
42	
43	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
44	
45	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped (correct only in hybrid), hurt (correct only in pure), or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, we verify below that it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). This binarization discards confidence magnitude---a transition from 0.51 to 0.49 is treated identically to one from 0.99 to 0.01---so we supplement it with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}).
46	
47	Each architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Table~\ref{tab:per_example}). GDN loses a net 153 examples (462 helped, 615 hurt), RetNet loses 69 (426/495), and HGRN2 gains 153 (548/395). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which attention produces a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\bar{J} \leq 0.104$ for helped, $\leq 0.096$ for hurt, indicating ${\sim}90$\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\text{null}} \approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), but normalizing by the theoretical upper bound $J_{\max} = \min(|A|,|B|)/\max(|A|,|B|)$, observed overlap reaches only 5--7\% of the achievable range above chance (normalized ES 0.065 helped, 0.047 hurt; per-pair details in Appendix~\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attributed to set-size constraints.
48	
49	No surface-level feature (token count, word frequency, syntactic complexity) reliably separates helped from hurt examples. However, pure-model confidence on the target token is strongly predictive within each architecture: hurt examples have lower target-token probability among pure-correct examples ($d = 0.83$--$0.92$), and helped examples have higher probability among pure-wrong examples ($d = 0.73$--$0.77$); see Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}. A Kruskal-Wallis test confirms cross-architecture structure in both hurt ($p = .0005$) and helped ($p = .020$) distributions. The confidence margin thus identifies a per-example predictor of which examples will be affected: attention disrupts marginally correct predictions and rescues near-miss errors (we use `rescue' and `disruption' as descriptive labels for observed transitions, not as causal claims about attention mechanisms), but with architecture-specific thresholds that produce the observed cross-architecture divergence. The rescue pattern generalizes: across all three architectures on LAMBADA, HellaSwag, and ARC-Easy, helped examples have significantly higher pure-model target probability than stayed-wrong examples (9/9 tests, $d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). The disruption pattern is weaker on ARC-Easy ($d = 0.13$--$0.26$), absent on HellaSwag under \texttt{acc\_norm}, and absent on binary classification (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}), likely reflecting structural properties of constrained-choice formats that limit the discriminative power of confidence margins. Full cross-benchmark continuous analysis, including per-architecture effect sizes on multiple-choice formats, appears in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}. This structured per-example disagreement despite similar aggregate performance constitutes predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} arising under architectural variation \citep[cf.][]{damour2022underspecification}.
50	
51	\paragraph{Multiplicity variation.} Extending the per-example analysis to HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$) reveals that predictive multiplicity varies systematically across tasks (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), forming a task-dependent variation. Evaluation format does not drive multiplicity: HellaSwag (four-way, $\alpha = 0.15$) and PIQA (two-choice, $\alpha = 0.14$) produce nearly identical multiplicity despite different formats; among two-choice benchmarks alone, $\alpha$ spans $3\times$ from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.41 (WinoGrande; Appendix~\ref{app:winogrande}) to 0.45 (BoolQ; Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}), covering the full six-benchmark range within a single format. Bootstrap 95\% CIs confirm the ordering: all adjacent-benchmark CIs are non-overlapping except PIQA and HellaSwag, whose CIs overlap substantially ($[.127,\,.159]$ vs.\ $[.141,\,.155]$; Appendix, Table~\ref{tab:arceasy_ambiguity}). Moreover, $\alpha$ does not significantly correlate with task accuracy (Spearman $\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$). Pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$ across four benchmarks), indicating that architectures affect different examples regardless of how many examples are affected. Within each benchmark, $\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$), but differs $2\times$ across task types (from reasoning to retrieval), confirming that multiplicity magnitude depends on task, not attention dose \citep[cf.][]{gorbett2026crossmodel}. The near-identical $\alpha$ for PIQA (0.14) and HellaSwag (0.15) despite different evaluation formats suggests a shared reasoning-task floor for multiplicity, while retrieval (LAMBADA, 0.30) and mixed (ARC-Easy, 0.21) tasks produce distinctly higher values.
52	
53	\begin{table}[t]
54	\centering
55	\small
56	\caption{Predictive multiplicity at 1.3B. $\alpha$: ambiguity at h3-1 \citep{marx2020predictive}. $\bar{J}$: mean pairwise Jaccard of helped~(h) and hurt~(u) sets, averaged across four hybrid attention ratios and three architecture pairs. $\rho_\alpha$: Spearman $\rho$ between attention ratio and $\alpha$. Benchmarks ordered by $\alpha$.}
57	\label{tab:multiplicity}
58	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrcccc@{}}
59	\toprule
60	 & $N$ & $\alpha$ & $\bar{J}_{h}$ & $\bar{J}_{u}$ & $\rho_\alpha$ \\
61	\midrule
62	PIQA & 1838 & .14 & .091 & .094 & $0.0$ \\
63	HellaSwag & 10042 & .15 & .095 & .085 & $0.1$ \\
64	ARC-Easy & 2376 & .21 & .092 & .096 & $-0.2$ \\
65	LAMBADA & 5153 & .30 & .086 & .091 & $-0.6$ \\
66	\bottomrule
67	\end{tabular}
68	\par\vspace{1pt}
69	{\footnotesize Within-benchmark $\alpha$ variation $< 0.03$ across ratios. BoolQ, WinoGrande: Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}, \ref{app:winogrande}.}
70	\end{table}
71	
72	\subsection{Decoupling}\label{ssec:decoupling}
73	
74	A key consequence of this multiplicity is that aggregate convergence and per-example divergence operate on different timescales as attention ratio increases (Figure~\ref{fig:decoupling}).
75	
76	Cross-architecture $\sigma$ decreases gradually across the five attention ratios: $0.024 \to 0.016 \to 0.014 \to 0.006 \to 0.005$. In contrast, pairwise Jaccard similarity between architectures' per-example effects saturates at the first attention level (4\%): approximately 0.095 for helped, with no further increase at higher ratios. Net asymmetry between architectures' per-example profiles increases from 0.013 at 4\% attention to 0.040 at 25\%. If convergence arose from attention uniformly rescuing the same difficult examples across architectures, Jaccard would increase in parallel with decreasing $\sigma$. The observed saturation of Jaccard at the first attention level indicates that each architecture's per-example response to attention is largely idiosyncratic. The observed $\bar{J} \approx 0.10$ is far below the theoretical maximum $J_{\max} \approx 0.78$--$0.92$ given helped-set sizes, ruling out ceiling constraints: there is ample room for Jaccard to increase if attention were homogenizing per-example behavior (normalized effect size 5--7\% of achievable range; \S\ref{ssec:per_example}).
77	
78	Bootstrap confidence intervals (10{,}000 resamples; heuristic given $n = 3$, \S\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}) support this pattern statistically: the 95\% CI for $\sigma$ does not overlap between pure $[.019,\,.030]$ and h3-1 $[.002,\,.011]$, while the CIs for mean Jaccard overlap completely across all non-zero attention ratios (helped: $[.08,\,.11]$; hurt: $[.07,\,.11]$). Within-architecture Jaccard averages ${\sim}0.33$ (Appendix~\ref{app:within_arch_stability}), roughly $3\times$ the cross-architecture value (${\sim}0.10$). This $3\times$ gap compares within-architecture variation across attention ratios to cross-architecture variation at matched ratios---a proxy for, not a direct measurement of, seed-versus-architecture effects. Attributing the gap entirely to seed variation would require seeds to exceed the effect of replacing 25\% of model layers with attention---implausible though not directly testable with the available data.
79	
80	Attention weight analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:attention_patterns}) shows GDN and RetNet produce nearly identical attention distributions (cosine 0.995) yet differ in per-example effects, suggesting these originate in the recurrent layers. The four-benchmark variation (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}) confirms independence: LAMBADA ($\alpha = 0.30$) is the only benchmark showing convergence despite having the highest multiplicity, while PIQA ($\alpha = 0.14$) and HellaSwag ($\alpha = 0.15$) show no convergence with the lowest multiplicity. Cross-scale analysis on all four benchmarks reveals that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity is scale-robust: pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$ at 340M vs.\ $0.09$--$0.095$ at 1.3B), and architecture-specific helped/hurt profiles persist. While $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M ($\Delta \leq 8$\,pp), this inflation does not correlate with accuracy loss across benchmarks (HellaSwag shows the largest accuracy gap yet one of the smallest $\alpha$ increases), suggesting capacity-related but not accuracy-driven modulation. At 340M, aggregate convergence is absent on all benchmarks ($\rho = -0.50$, $p = .45$ for LAMBADA). This dissociation suggests that the \emph{pattern} of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---reflects structural differences in recurrent processing, while absolute $\alpha$ magnitude is partially modulated by model capacity (Appendix~\ref{app:scale_per_example}).
81	
82	\subsection{Robustness}\label{ssec:robustness}
83	
84	Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\rho = -1.0$ to $\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$), partly because GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves the decoupling: all hybrid $\sigma \leq 0.008$ while Jaccard saturates at 0.086--0.108. The aggregate convergence result is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. Full sensitivity analyses appear in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}.
85	
```

Ratio: $\\sigma_{\\text{pure}} / \\sigma_{\\text{h3-1}}$. \\textbf{Bold}: only benchmark with $\\rho = -1.0$.}\n\\label{tab:convergence}\n\\begin{tabular}{llrrrrr}\n\\toprule\nBenchmark & Category & $\\rho$ & $p$ & $\\sigma_{\\text{pure}}$ & $\\sigma_{\\text{h3-1}}$ & Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{LAMBADA$^{\\ddagger}$} & \\textbf{Retrieval} & $\\mathbf{-1.0}$ & \\textbf{.017} & \\textbf{2.41} & \\textbf{0.49} & \\textbf{4.87} \\\\\nBoolQ & Comprehension & $0.2$ & .783 & 1.63 & 2.36 & 0.69 \\\\\nARC-C & Reasoning & $0.6$ & .350 & 0.96 & 1.32 & 0.72 \\\\\nHellaSwag & Reasoning & $0.4$ & .517 & 1.17 & 1.22 & 0.96 \\\\\nPIQA & Reasoning & $-0.5$ & .450 & 0.54 & 0.34 & 1.59 \\\\\nWinoGrande & Reasoning & $0.1$ & .950 & 0.67 & 0.78 & 0.86 \\\\\nARC-E & Mixed & $-0.7$ & .233 & 1.00 & 0.13 & 7.70 \\\\\nMMLU & Mixed & $-0.7$ & .233 & 0.92 & 0.41 & 2.27 \\\\\nSciQ & Mixed & $0.1$ & .950 & 0.87 & 0.74 & 1.19 \\\\\nXStoryCloze & Mixed & $-0.6$ & .350 & 0.82 & 0.55 & 1.49 \\\\\n\\midrule\nCOPA$^{\\dagger}$ & Reasoning & $-0.67$ & .267 & 2.63 & 0.47 & 5.57 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\footnotesize $^{\\dagger}$Supplementary ($n = 100$); excluded from multiple comparison correction.} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\footnotesize $^{\\ddagger}$GDN h3-1 uses 16 attention heads; all other configurations use 32 (\\S\\ref{ssec:robustness}).} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\footnotesize Bonferroni-adjusted $p = 6 \\times 2/120 = 0.100$ (exact); 0.102 arises from rounding raw $p$ to .017 before multiplying.}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\\label{ssec:per_example}\n\nDo architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped (correct only in hybrid), hurt (correct only in pure), or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, we verify below that it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\\ref{app:cm_cross}). This binarization discards confidence magnitude---a transition from 0.51 to 0.49 is treated identically to one from 0.99 to 0.01---so we supplement it with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:confidence_margin}).\n\nEach architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Table~\\ref{tab:per_example}). GDN loses a net 153 examples (462 helped, 615 hurt), RetNet loses 69 (426/495), and HGRN2 gains 153 (548/395). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which attention produces a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\\bar{J} \\leq 0.104$ for helped, $\\leq 0.096$ for hurt, indicating ${\\sim}90$\\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\\text{null}} \\approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), but normalizing by the theoretical upper bound $J_{\\max} = \\min(|A|,|B|)/\\max(|A|,|B|)$, observed overlap reaches only 5--7\\% of the achievable range above chance (normalized ES 0.065 helped, 0.047 hurt; per-pair details in Appendix~\\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attributed to set-size constraints.\n\nNo surface-level feature (token count, word frequency, syntactic complexity) reliably separates helped from hurt examples. However, pure-model confidence on the target token is strongly predictive within each architecture: hurt examples have lower target-token probability among pure-correct examples ($d = 0.83$--$0.92$), and helped examples have higher probability among pure-wrong examples ($d = 0.73$--$0.77$); see Appendix~\\ref{app:confidence_margin}. A Kruskal-Wallis test confirms cross-architecture structure in both hurt ($p = .0005$) and helped ($p = .020$) distributions. The confidence margin thus identifies a per-example predictor of which examples will be affected: attention disrupts marginally correct predictions and rescues near-miss errors (we use `rescue' and `disruption' as descriptive labels for observed transitions, not as causal claims about attention mechanisms), but with architecture-specific thresholds that produce the observed cross-architecture divergence. The rescue pattern generalizes: across all three architectures on LAMBADA, HellaSwag, and ARC-Easy, helped examples have significantly higher pure-model target probability than stayed-wrong examples (9/9 tests, $d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cm_cross}). The disruption pattern is weaker on ARC-Easy ($d = 0.13$--$0.26$), absent on HellaSwag under \\texttt{acc\\_norm}, and absent on binary classification (Appendix~\\ref{app:boolq}), likely reflecting structural properties of constrained-choice formats that limit the discriminative power of confidence margins. Full cross-benchmark continuous analysis, including per-architecture effect sizes on multiple-choice formats, appears in Appendix~\\ref{app:cm_cross}. This structured per-example disagreement despite similar aggregate performance constitutes predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} arising under architectural variation \\citep[cf.][]{damour2022underspecification}.\n\n\\paragraph{Multiplicity variation.} Extending the per-example analysis to HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$) reveals that predictive multiplicity varies systematically across tasks (Table~\\ref{tab:multiplicity}). The ambiguity $\\alpha$ \\citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), forming a task-dependent variation. Evaluation format does not drive multiplicity: HellaSwag (four-way, $\\alpha = 0.15$) and PIQA (two-choice, $\\alpha = 0.14$) produce nearly identical multiplicity despite different formats; among two-choice benchmarks alone, $\\alpha$ spans $3\\times$ from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.41 (WinoGrande; Appendix~\\ref{app:winogrande}) to 0.45 (BoolQ; Appendix~\\ref{app:boolq}), covering the full six-benchmark range within a single format. Bootstrap 95\\% CIs confirm the ordering: all adjacent-benchmark CIs are non-overlapping except PIQA and HellaSwag, whose CIs overlap substantially ($[.127,\\,.159]$ vs.\\ $[.141,\\,.155]$; Appendix, Table~\\ref{tab:arceasy_ambiguity}). Moreover, $\\alpha$ does not significantly correlate with task accuracy (Spearman $\\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$). Pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\\bar{J} \\leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$ across four benchmarks), indicating that architectures affect different examples regardless of how many examples are affected. Within each benchmark, $\\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$), but differs $2\\times$ across task types (from reasoning to retrieval), confirming that multiplicity magnitude depends on task, not attention dose \\citep[cf.][]{gorbett2026crossmodel}. The near-identical $\\alpha$ for PIQA (0.14) and HellaSwag (0.15) despite different evaluation formats suggests a shared reasoning-task floor for multiplicity, while retrieval (LAMBADA, 0.30) and mixed (ARC-Easy, 0.21) tasks produce distinctly higher values.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Predictive multiplicity at 1.3B. $\\alpha$: ambiguity at h3-1 \\citep{marx2020predictive}. $\\bar{J}$: mean pairwise Jaccard of helped~(h) and hurt~(u) sets, averaged across four hybrid attention ratios and three architecture pairs. $\\rho_\\alpha$: Spearman $\\rho$ between attention ratio and $\\alpha$. Benchmarks ordered by $\\alpha$.}\n\\label{tab:multiplicity}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lrcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n & $N$ & $\\alpha$ & $\\bar{J}_{h}$ & $\\bar{J}_{u}$ & $\\rho_\\alpha$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nPIQA & 1838 & .14 & .091 & .094 & $0.0$ \\\\\nHellaSwag & 10042 & .15 & .095 & .085 & $0.1$ \\\\\nARC-Easy & 2376 & .21 & .092 & .096 & $-0.2$ \\\\\nLAMBADA & 5153 & .30 & .086 & .091 & $-0.6$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\par\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize Within-benchmark $\\alpha$ variation $< 0.03$ across ratios. BoolQ, WinoGrande: Appendix~\\ref{app:boolq}, \\ref{app:winogrande}.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Decoupling}\\label{ssec:decoupling}\n\nA key consequence of this multiplicity is that aggregate convergence and per-example divergence operate on different timescales as attention ratio increases (Figure~\\ref{fig:decoupling}).\n\nCross-architecture $\\sigma$ decreases gradually across the five attention ratios: $0.024 \\to 0.016 \\to 0.014 \\to 0.006 \\to 0.005$. In contrast, pairwise Jaccard similarity between architectures' per-example effects saturates at the first attention level (4\\%): approximately 0.095 for helped, with no further increase at higher ratios. Net asymmetry between architectures' per-example profiles increases from 0.013 at 4\\% attention to 0.040 at 25\\%. If convergence arose from attention uniformly rescuing the same difficult examples across architectures, Jaccard would increase in parallel with decreasing $\\sigma$. The observed saturation of Jaccard at the first attention level indicates that each architecture's per-example response to attention is largely idiosyncratic. The observed $\\bar{J} \\approx 0.10$ is far below the theoretical maximum $J_{\\max} \\approx 0.78$--$0.92$ given helped-set sizes, ruling out ceiling constraints: there is ample room for Jaccard to increase if attention were homogenizing per-example behavior (normalized effect size 5--7\\% of achievable range; \\S\\ref{ssec:per_example}).\n\nBootstrap confidence intervals (10{,}000 resamples; heuristic given $n = 3$, \\S\\ref{ssec:statistical_methods}) support this pattern statistically: the 95\\% CI for $\\sigma$ does not overlap between pure $[.019,\\,.030]$ and h3-1 $[.002,\\,.011]$, while the CIs for mean Jaccard overlap completely across all non-zero attention ratios (helped: $[.08,\\,.11]$; hurt: $[.07,\\,.11]$). Within-architecture Jaccard averages ${\\sim}0.33$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:within_arch_stability}), roughly $3\\times$ the cross-architecture value (${\\sim}0.10$). This $3\\times$ gap compares within-architecture variation across attention ratios to cross-architecture variation at matched ratios---a proxy for, not a direct measurement of, seed-versus-architecture effects. Attributing the gap entirely to seed variation would require seeds to exceed the effect of replacing 25\\% of model layers with attention---implausible though not directly testable with the available data.\n\nAttention weight analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:attention_patterns}) shows GDN and RetNet produce nearly identical attention distributions (cosine 0.995) yet differ in per-example effects, suggesting these originate in the recurrent layers. The four-benchmark variation (Table~\\ref{tab:multiplicity}) confirms independence: LAMBADA ($\\alpha = 0.30$) is the only benchmark showing convergence despite having the highest multiplicity, while PIQA ($\\alpha = 0.14$) and HellaSwag ($\\alpha = 0.15$) show no convergence with the lowest multiplicity. Cross-scale analysis on all four benchmarks reveals that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity is scale-robust: pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\\bar{J} = 0.08$--$0.10$ at 340M vs.\\ $0.09$--$0.095$ at 1.3B), and architecture-specific helped/hurt profiles persist. While $\\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M ($\\Delta \\leq 8$\\,pp), this inflation does not correlate with accuracy loss across benchmarks (HellaSwag shows the largest accuracy gap yet one of the smallest $\\alpha$ increases), suggesting capacity-related but not accuracy-driven modulation. At 340M, aggregate convergence is absent on all benchmarks ($\\rho = -0.50$, $p = .45$ for LAMBADA). This dissociation suggests that the \\emph{pattern} of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---reflects structural differences in recurrent processing, while absolute $\\alpha$ magnitude is partially modulated by model capacity (Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_per_example}).\n\n\\subsection{Robustness}\\label{ssec:robustness}\n\nRemoving GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\\rho = -1.0$ to $\\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$), partly because GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves the decoupling: all hybrid $\\sigma \\leq 0.008$ while Jaccard saturates at 0.086--0.108. The aggregate convergence result is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. Full sensitivity analyses appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}.\n",

      "totalLines": 85
    }

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We present the first systematic per-example analysis of attention effects in hybrid recurrent models. Per-example multiplicity spans a task-dependent variation ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) across four benchmarks, with Jaccard uniformly low ($\leq 0.10$) regardless of aggregate convergence. On LAMBADA, aggregate variance decreases with attention while per-example overlap saturates, suggesting architecture-specific mechanisms rather than uniform rescue; this observation is dependent on GDN inclusion and does not replicate on other benchmarks.
4	
5	For practitioners selecting among hybrid architectures, these results suggest a concrete protocol. Per-example audits are warranted whenever multiple hybrid designs achieve comparable aggregate scores on a target task: the low Jaccard ($\leq 0.10$, indicating ${\sim}90\%$ non-overlap) means that architectures which appear interchangeable by aggregate metrics affect nearly disjoint sets of examples. In deployment contexts where prediction consistency matters---medical, legal, or safety-critical applications---aggregate equivalence does not imply per-example equivalence, and architecture choice may determine which specific inputs are handled correctly. A proof-of-concept majority vote across three architectures does not outperform the best individual architecture (LAMBADA: $-0.2$\,pp; ARC-Easy: $-1.1$\,pp), despite 15--45\% per-example disagreement. Oracle accuracy---correct if any architecture is correct---reveals substantial complementarity potential ($+7$--$14$\,pp above best), but majority vote captures only 1--9\% of this ceiling. The failure of majority vote does not negate the value of per-example information: the oracle gap ($+7$--$14$\,pp) confirms that complementary knowledge exists across architectures, but accessing it requires selection methods more sophisticated than naive ensembling.
6	
```

  "old_string": "Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that interleave attention with recurrence are increasingly deployed, yet how attention affects per-example behavior across backbones remains unquantified. Using the controlled model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}---three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales (340M/1.3B), single training runs per configuration---we evaluate 10 benchmarks and decompose per-example effects on four (LAMBADA, HellaSwag, PIQA, ARC-Easy). Per-example predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} varies across tasks ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) while pairwise Jaccard of helped/hurt sets remains uniformly low ($\\leq 0.10$); multiplicity magnitude depends on task type, not attention dose. Aggregate convergence, by contrast, is task-specific: only LAMBADA provides exploratory evidence for monotonic variance reduction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$), consistent with attention compensating for retrieval differences across recurrent backbones. On LAMBADA, aggregate variance decreases gradually while per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level---the two are decoupled.",
  "new_string": "When attention layers are added to linear recurrent architectures, they help and hurt almost entirely different examples across backbones: pairwise overlap among three controlled hybrid designs is at most 10\\%, far below the 78--92\\% theoretical maximum. Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales, single training runs per configuration), we decompose per-example effects on four benchmarks and find that this predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$ for reasoning, $0.30$ for retrieval) rather than attention dose. Aggregate convergence is task-specific: only LAMBADA provides exploratory evidence for monotonic variance reduction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .100$), and on this benchmark, aggregate variance decreases gradually while per-example overlap saturates at the first attention level. A proof-of-concept majority vote across architectures fails to outperform the best individual model despite 15--45\\% per-example disagreement, while oracle accuracy reveals 7--14\\,pp of untapped complementarity, suggesting that architectures interchangeable by aggregate metrics may carry hidden consequences for per-example reliability."
}
